Source

Issue Title
No main landmark defined on the page
Page name
Global
Recommendation for Fix
Use <main> </main> tag to define the main region for the page.
Sr. #
1
Steps to Reproduce
1. Enable JAWS and launch chrome Browser. 2. Load the page https://pristineai.com/ 3. User will be redirected to the 'Home' page. 4. Press 'R' key to navigate to all the regions present on the page. 5. Observe that "Main" region is not present.
Type of Change
Code
WCAG Sc
1.3.1
actual result
The main region is not defined programmatically on the page. As a result, screen reader users might not be able to identify the primary content of the page affectively.
comment
element name

expected result
Screen reader users should be able to identify each landmark region as well as navigate to different regions on the page.

Run ID: 20261029T234356-7967030b-page-001-9f11a1cf

Eligibility classification
Programmatic checks
Recorded explanation
LAND_001 checks the absence of a main landmark; every saved page has the reported missing main region.
Human finding mapping approved by
Fresh audit evidence review (Codex, 2026-10-22)
Approved Programmatic explanation
The selected LAND_001 findings identify the reported elements on this page and jointly cover the whole Human defect. LAND_001 checks the absence of a main landmark; every saved page has the reported missing main region.
LLM matching rationale
The landmark finding states that no landmark covers the primary content and recommends wrapping that content in main, matching the missing main region.
VA-DAT Matched Findings
Snapshot has header, two nav elements and footer but no main or role=main.

LLM Finding

LLM (landmark structure check): Structural imbalance: every landmark (header, two navs, footer) covers page chrome, and none covers the primary content. Screen reader users who move by landmark can jump to banner, navigation, or footer but cannot reach the article, product, or form the page exists for. An automated check can flag a missing <main>, but only a person can decide which region is the primary content and where its edges belong, such as whether a sidebar or page-level <h1> belongs inside it.

LAND_001 checks the absence of a main landmark; every saved page has the reported missing main region.
Human finding mapping approved by
Fresh audit evidence review (Codex, 2026-10-22)
Approved Programmatic explanation
The selected LAND_001 findings identify the reported elements on this page and jointly cover the whole Human defect. LAND_001 checks the absence of a main landmark; every saved page has the reported missing main region.
LLM matching rationale
The landmark finding identifies that the primary page content has no main landmark and recommends wrapping that content in main, matching the human missing-main defect.
VA-DAT Matched Findings
The snapshot has header, two nav elements and footer, with no main element or role=main. This is an LLM cross-catch of the approved programmatic finding.

LLM Finding

LLM (landmark structure check): Structural imbalance: The landmarks cover only peripheral page regions (header, two navigation regions, footer). Screen reader users moving by landmark can reach every part of the site's navigation but have no landmark for the page's primary content. This leaves navigation overrepresented relative to content. (A missing <main> can be flagged automatically. The judgement here is that the overall landmark map gives no route to what the page is actually about.)

Original Human finding

Human finding ID
human-34fa811c363f72c6
Source
Defect report, row 4; scope: global; element: Region
Browser Combination
Chrome + JAWS
Issue Title
Missing accessible name for navigation region
Page name
Global
Recommendation for Fix
Specify unique and descriptive accessible name such as "Primary" using aria-label attribute.
Sr. #
3
Steps to Reproduce
1. Enable JAWS and launch chrome Browser. 2. Load the page https://pristineai.com/ 3. User will be redirected to the 'Home' page. 4. Press 'R' key to navigate to all the regions present on the page. 5. Observe that accessible name is not defined for the navigation region.
Type of Change
Code
WCAG Sc
1.3.1
actual result
Accessible name is missing for the navigation region containing "Solutions", "CLIENT", "ABOUT" and so on links present in the header region. As a result, screen reader users will find it difficult to understand the purpose of the navigation region.
comment
element name
Region
expected result
The navigation region should be identified with unique and appropriate name using screen reader for their users.

Human finding mapping approved by
Fresh audit evidence review (Codex, 2026-10-22)
Approved Programmatic explanation
The selected LAND_005 findings identify the reported elements on this page and jointly cover the whole Human defect. LAND_005 reports both unlabeled header navigation regions on each saved page. Both findings jointly cover the reported navigation region naming defect.
LLM matching rationale
The finding identifies unnamed navigation landmarks and their indistinguishable navigation announcements, matching the unnamed header navigation.
VA-DAT Matched Findings
Both nav elements are in the header and contain the Solutions, Clients and About menu. The response speculates about a secondary menu, but correctly detects the absent accessible names on the reported menu.

LLM Finding

LLM (landmark structure check): Ambiguous navigation purpose: the two <nav> landmarks have no names, so both are announced simply as 'navigation'. A user browsing the landmarks list cannot tell which one holds the site menu and which holds secondary links. A tool can flag duplicate unnamed landmarks, but only a person can choose names that describe each nav's real purpose. Screen readers already announce the 'navigation' role, so the names should be short and should not repeat the word.

Approved Programmatic explanation
The selected LAND_005 findings identify the reported elements on this page and jointly cover the whole Human defect. LAND_005 reports both unlabeled header navigation regions on each saved page. Both findings jointly cover the reported navigation region naming defect.
LLM matching rationale

VA-DAT Matched Findings

LLM Finding

LLM (landmark structure check): Ambiguous navigation regions: Two navigation landmarks exist with nothing to tell them apart. In a landmark list, users will hear 'navigation, navigation' and cannot tell which is the primary site menu and which is secondary (for example breadcrumb, sub-section, utility, or footer links) without entering each one. Good labels depend on what each menu actually does, which requires human review of the page.

Missing unordered list mark-up1.3.1GlobalLLMNot caughtNot caught

Original Human finding

Human finding ID
human-7c0c4f4e692f8672
Source
Defect report, row 5; scope: global; element: List
Browser Combination
Chrome + JAWS
Issue Title
Missing unordered list mark-up
Page name
Global
Recommendation for Fix
Use appropriate list mark-up, such as <ul> and <li> to identify related information. In addition, consider applying styles to maintain the visual presentation of a web page.
Sr. #
4
Steps to Reproduce
1. Enable JAWS and launch chrome Browser. 2. Load the page https://pristineai.com/ 3. User will be redirected to the 'Home' page. 4. Navigate to the mentioned links present in the footer region. 5. Inspect the code and observe that list mark-up is missing for the links.
Type of Change
Code
WCAG Sc
1.3.1
actual result
List mark-up is not used to present related information programatically. For example, Links such as "About", "Knowledge", "Our team" and "Everyday Pricing", "Personalized Offers", "Supplier Collaboration" and so on is not marked as unordered list which visually looks like a list of related items. As a result, screen reader users will find it difficult to navigate to the list and different list items quickly.
comment
element name
List
expected result
Related group of items should be identified as list by screen readers. This will help users to understand how many items are present and quickly navigate to different items of the list.

Headings not defined1.3.1GlobalLLMNot caughtNot caught

View details

Original Human finding

Human finding ID
human-239db6c68014b97f
Source
Defect report, row 6; scope: global; element: Heading
Browser Combination
Chrome + JAWS
Issue Title
Headings not defined
Page name
Global
Recommendation for Fix
Mark-up the mentioned text using appropriate HTML headings. In addition, use heading levels as per specification such as <h1> should be followed by <h2>, <h3> and so on.
Sr. #
5
Steps to Reproduce
1. Enable JAWS and launch chrome Browser. 2. Load the page https://pristineai.com/ 3. User will be redirected to the 'Home' page. 4. Navigate to the mentioned texts present in the footer region. 5. Inspect the code and observe that the text is not defined as a heading.
Type of Change
Code
WCAG Sc
1.3.1
actual result
The text such as "Pristine", "Solutions", and so on present in the footer region visually appears to be heading on the page. This text is not marked-up using HTML heading. This resulted in screen reader users finding it difficult to understand the page content structure.
comment
element name
Heading
expected result
Screen reader should identify the text as a heading for their users.

There is no adjacent text such as a visible email address or 'Email' label, so the icon may be the only cue that contact information is available. It would be decorative only if a visible email address or label sits right next to it. If it is wrapped in a mailto link elsewhere in the module, it is functional and must have a text alternative.

VA-DAT Matched Findings

It nevertheless explicitly identifies the same informative mail image and its missing alternative, supported by the human audit. Its omission of removing title does not change detection of the underlying defect.

LLM Finding

LLM (decorative verification check): The filename suggests an envelope/email icon. It sits in a standalone Divi image module in a job listing for 'Senior Azure Cloud Engineer' and has no adjacent text. Icons like this on job pages usually mean 'email us' or 'apply by email'. With no visible label next to it, the icon may be the only cue that an email contact or application option exists. If it is wrapped in a mailto: link (common for Divi image modules with a link URL), a missing text alternative leaves the link with no accessible name. Even if it is not linked, it likely signals that contact or application information follows.

llm-a883c8eaeeb606c838d5
Check
decorative_verification; CL03
Element
<img src="mail.png" alt="">
Location
In the main job listing content under the 'Senior Azure Cloud Engineer' heading, inside a Divi image module column

LLM matching rationale
The finding identifies mail.png in the job content as potentially informative rather than decorative and needing a meaningful alternative when it conveys email or application information. The snapshot confirms the same unlinked image above the job introduction has alt=""; the human audit supplies the informative-image context.
VA-DAT Matched Findings
Qualified match under the practical semantic rule for tentative findings. The response has low confidence and asks whether nearby text makes the image decorative. The saved image is in its own column, and the human finding explicitly calls it informative. The response also describes extracted alt as null, while the snapshot contains alt="". The missing informative alternative remains the same defect.

LLM Finding

LLM (decorative verification check): The filename suggests an envelope/email icon. It appears in a job posting for 'Senior .NET Engineer' with no adjacent text. On a job listing, a standalone mail icon often signals how to apply (by email) or marks a contact address. With no visible text next to it, the icon may be the only thing conveying that meaning. If an email address or 'Apply by email' label sits right next to it in the layout, it would be decorative. That cannot be confirmed from the data, so the image needs human review. Also note that alt is reported as null rather than an empty string, so the image may not actually be marked as decorative.

Human finding mapping approved by
Fresh audit evidence review (Codex, 2026-10-22)
Approved Programmatic explanation
The unlinked Mail image on Principal AI Scientist has alt="" and title="mail". NON_TEXT_001 only catches missing attributes and NON_TEXT_002 requires a link; deciding that this image conveys information belongs to the LLM.
LLM matching rationale
The finding identifies mail.png at the job introduction as potentially informative email/application content without a text alternative, matching the audited informative Mail image with empty alt.
VA-DAT Matched Findings
Qualified semantic match. Snapshot confirms the exact unlinked wp-image-1006 Mail image with alt="" and title="mail" near the job heading. The model speculates that it might be linked and requests confirmation of purpose. Its nondecorative verdict and missing-alternative diagnosis apply to this image, and the human audit supplies the informative-purpose context.

LLM Finding

LLM (decorative verification check): The filename suggests an email/envelope icon on a job posting for a Principal AI Scientist. On job pages, a mail icon usually signals how to apply or whom to contact. There is no adjacent text, so the icon may be the only cue for that action. Divi image modules are often wrapped in a link (for example a mailto: link). If so, an image with no text alternative leaves the link without an accessible name. It would only be decorative if visible text next to it already says something like 'Email us' or shows the address.

Finding ID
llm-b98d43d8fa8e4174082a
Check
decorative_verification; CL03
Element
<img src="mail.png" alt="">
Location
In the 'Principal AI Scientist' job posting section, within a content column near that heading
